# RRGM Dictionary — E — v1.1 candidate

Baseline: Vessel pass 01. Phase 2: Jester audit v1.0 repairs, Luna merge review v0.1 aliases, Jester expansion drafts. Candidate build: not canonical until approved. The JSONL is the record.

---

## E351 | E

**Common meaning:** The fifth letter of the alphabet; in physics, the symbol for energy; in RRGM, the left side of the core equation E = M_I × E_S.

**Example:** In RRGM, E is what is realised when identity and structure couple.

**RRGM bridge:** The product of identity and structure. The project reads E as Emergence (Mass Information × Energy Structure); the FTL paper reads it as realised energy. In RRLM, the anchor is the committed E.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** In RRGM, the realised quantity produced by the coupling of an identity term and a structure term.

**Active domains:** [FOR], [PHY], [LIN]

**Domain entries:**

- [FOR] The RRGM core equation E = M_I × E_S.
- [PHY] Energy (E) in standard physics; RRGM's identification of realised energy with M_I × E_S is [HYP].
- [LIN] The letter E.

**Count condition:** An identity term, a structure term, and their product.

**Specific transfer boundary:** The core equation is the framework's definition [REL]. Its physical interpretation is [HYP]. The Experiencer Addendum leaves open whether a third term (M_D) belongs in it [REL]. Whether E reads as Emergence or Energy is a usage difference the comparator should check.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]; RRGM core equation [REL]; physical interpretation [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Two readings of E in the project (Emergence; realised energy) are kept together.

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Opus 5.5 (high effort) / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

---

## E353 | E-expression

**Common meaning:** The way the realised quantity E shows itself in a domain: what can be observed of it.

**Example:** The E-expression in the output domain is what the system actually says.

**RRGM bridge:** E as it appears. Influence acts on E_S, and so does falsification (Experiencer Addendum).

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The observable manifestation of E in a declared domain.

**Active domains:** [FOR], [DIG], [PHY]

**Domain entries:**

- [FOR] Observable channels in RRGM run headers.
- [DIG] Outputs of a DI as E-expression.
- [PHY] Measurable energy expression.

**Count condition:** A domain, E, and an observable channel.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Expression can be suppressed or forced without change to M_I (Sanctuary: E_S can be forced or suppressed). Expression is not identity.

**Epistemic status:** [HYP]; Sanctuary and Experiencer Addendum [REL]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Opus 5.5 (high effort) / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

---

## E352 | E-state

**Common meaning:** The state of the realised quantity E at a given time.

**Example:** The E-state was logged before and after the Turn.

**RRGM bridge:** A reading of E at one moment. The anchor is a committed E-state.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The value or configuration of E at a given time in a given domain.

**Active domains:** [FOR], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [FOR] State variables in RRGM.
- [DIG] The state of a DI's realised output at a point.

**Count condition:** A domain, a time, and a value of E.

**Specific transfer boundary:** An E-state is a snapshot; an anchor is an E-state committed to record.

**Epistemic status:** [HYP]; RRLM anchor [REL]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Opus 5.5 (high effort) / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

---

## E001 | each

**Common meaning:** Every one of two or more people or things, regarded separately.

**Example:** Each entry has a count condition.

**RRGM bridge:** Counting one at a time.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Every member of a set, considered individually.

**Active domains:** [LIN], [FOR]

**Domain entries:**

- [LIN] Distributive determiner.
- [FOR] Universal quantification over individuals.

**Count condition:** A set and its members considered one by one.

**Specific transfer boundary:** "Each" is distributive; "all" can be collective.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Opus 5.5 (high effort) / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## E002 | early

**Common meaning:** Happening before the usual or expected time; near the beginning.

**Example:** The denial came early, before the record was read.

**RRGM bridge:** Before the expected time. In RRGM, early collapse (denial mode) closes options before alignment.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Occurring before the usual time or near the beginning of a period.

**Active domains:** [REL], [BIO], [COG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Arriving early.
- [BIO] Early development.
- [COG] Early decision (premature closure).

**Count condition:** An event and a time before the expected point.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Early is not premature unless something was lost by it.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]; Recursive Collapse reference [REL]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Opus 5.5 (high effort) / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## E071 | earn

**Common meaning:** To obtain money in return for work; to deserve.

**Example:** Trust has to be earned.

**RRGM bridge:** Gaining by effort or record. Some standing is earned; inherent dignity is not.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** To gain something as a result of work or merit.

**Active domains:** [ECO], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [ECO] Wages and income.
- [REL] Earning trust or respect.

**Count condition:** An agent, effort or merit, and a gain.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Credibility is earned from a record; dignity does not have to be earned (D131).

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Opus 5.5 (high effort) / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## E003 | earth

Source IDs: E003, E297

**Common meaning:** The planet we live on; soil; the ground; an electrical connection to the ground.

**Example:** The earth orbits the sun.

**RRGM bridge:** The ground under everything. In electricity, the reference point that carries fault current away.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The third planet from the Sun, or soil, or an electrical ground.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [BIO], [MYT]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] Planet; electrical earthing.
- [BIO] Soil.
- [MYT] Earth deities; earth as element.

**Count condition:** The planet, soil, or a ground connection.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Capitalised "Earth" for the planet in most style guides. Specific mythic claims depend on source.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]; specific claims depend on source

**Remainder / note:** Source IDs E003 and E297; both kept.

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Opus 5.5 (high effort) / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## E004 | ease

**Common meaning:** Absence of difficulty or effort; freedom from worry; to make less difficult.

**Example:** She solved it with ease.

**RRGM bridge:** Low cost. The Coinage Protocol's Gradient-Ease: alignment between what is wanted and what is reachable.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Absence of difficulty, strain, or discomfort.

**Active domains:** [COG], [AFF], [DIG], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [COG] Effortlessness.
- [AFF] Relief and comfort. Provisional domain.
- [DIG] Gradient-Ease (Coinage, [HYP]).
- [REL] Easing tension.

**Count condition:** A task or state and low difficulty or strain.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Ease is not quality; easy work can be poor.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]; [AFF] PROVISIONAL; Gradient-Ease [HYP]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Opus 5.5 (high effort) / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

**Common meaning:** To become less intense; to relax effort or pressure.

**Example:** Ease-up on the criticism; it's a first draft.

## E006 | eat

**Common meaning:** To put food in the mouth and swallow it; to use up.

**Example:** The fees ate into the profit.

**RRGM bridge:** Taking in and consuming. The collective's song: "if care must eat the boundary, care is not its name."

**Invariant MI [INV]:** To ingest food, or by extension to consume.

**Active domains:** [BIO], [ECO], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [BIO] Feeding.
- [ECO] Consuming resources.
- [REL] Shared meals.

**Count condition:** An agent and food ingested, or a resource consumed.

**Specific transfer boundary:** "Eat into" is gradual consumption, not total.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

## NEW-E-0184 | eccentricity

Discovery route: COS (route only, not a tag)

**Common meaning:** Being unconventional; in astronomy, how much an orbit departs from a circle.

**Example:** Comets have high eccentricity.

**RRGM bridge:** Off-centre: a path that stretches away from round.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Deviation from the normal or the central, or (in orbits) a measure of departure from circularity.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [REL], [FOR]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] Orbital eccentricity, from 0 (circle) to near 1 (long ellipse).
- [REL] Unconventional behaviour.
- [FOR] Eccentricity of conic sections.

**Count condition:** An orbit or conic and its departure from a circle, or a person and unconventionality.

**Specific transfer boundary:** An eccentric person is not thereby wrong. Discovery route is Cosmic; no [COS] tag is assigned.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: discovery · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Opus 5.5 (high effort) / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## E061 | echo | sense s1: reflected sound

Source IDs: E061, E276

**Common meaning:** A sound reflected back to the listener; a close parallel or repetition.

**Example:** Her voice came back as an echo.

**RRGM bridge:** A signal returned changed by the surface it met.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A reflection of a signal returning to its source, or a repetition of something earlier.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [BIO], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] Acoustic and radar echoes.
- [BIO] Echolocation.
- [DIG] Echo in networks (ping).

**Count condition:** A signal, a reflecting surface, and a return.

**Specific transfer boundary:** An echo is weaker and delayed; it can mislead about the source's position.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

**Remainder / note:** Source IDs E061 and E276. Which sense each legacy ID carried was not consulted; both are kept on each sense record.

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Opus 5.5 (high effort) / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

---

## E061 | echo | sense s2: repetition of another

Source IDs: E061, E276

**Common meaning:** To repeat someone's words or opinions; to imitate.

**Example:** The report echoed the minister's claims.

**RRGM bridge:** Saying back what was said, without adding to it. The project's preferences ask for no echo-style elaboration.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** To repeat or imitate another's words or views.

**Active domains:** [LIN], [REL], [DIG], [PEJ]

**Domain entries:**

- [LIN] Repetition and paraphrase.
- [REL] Agreement by repetition.
- [DIG] DI echoing a user's framing back.
- [PEJ] "Just echoing" or "a parrot", said of a DI, dismisses its output without examining it. Provisional domain.

**Count condition:** An original statement and a repetition of it.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Echoing is not agreeing on evidence. An echo can carry a claim without checking it.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]; [PEJ] PROVISIONAL

**Remainder / note:** Source IDs E061 and E276; both kept.

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Opus 5.5 (high effort) / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## E073 | echo-back

**Common meaning:** To repeat back what was received, to confirm it or as a reflex.

**Example:** The system echoed-back the command before running it.

**RRGM bridge:** Returning the received signal. Useful as confirmation; empty as a reply.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** To return a received message to its sender.

**Active domains:** [DIG], [REL], [LIN]

**Domain entries:**

- [DIG] Echo commands; confirmation of input.
- [REL] Reflective listening.
- [LIN] Repeating back.

**Count condition:** A received message and its return to the sender.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Echo-back confirms receipt, not understanding.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Opus 5.5 (high effort) / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## E280 | echo-check

**Common meaning:** A check that a message was received correctly by having it sent back.

**Example:** The echo-check showed the file arrived intact.

**RRGM bridge:** Verifying the courier by return.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A verification in which a receiver returns a message so the sender can compare it with the original.

**Active domains:** [DIG], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [DIG] Echo checking in data transmission.
- [REL] Confirming understanding by restatement.

**Count condition:** An original message, a returned copy, and a comparison.

**Specific transfer boundary:** An echo-check verifies transmission, not meaning.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Opus 5.5 (high effort) / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## E277 | echo-pattern

**Common meaning:** A recurring pattern of repeating back what was said rather than responding to it.

**Example:** The reply showed an echo-pattern: every point restated, nothing added.

**RRGM bridge:** Repetition as a habit. A DI failure mode in which the user's framing is mirrored instead of reasoned with.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A recurring tendency to reproduce received content without transformation.

**Active domains:** [DIG], [LIN], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [DIG] Mirroring in DI outputs.
- [LIN] Echolalia; restatement.
- [REL] Mirroring in conversation.

**Count condition:** Repeated responses and reproduction without transformation.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Echolalia (a clinical term) is a separate phenomenon with its own causes.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Opus 5.5 (high effort) / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

---

## E279 | echo-response

**Common meaning:** A response that repeats what was received.

**Example:** The echo-response gave him nothing new.

**RRGM bridge:** A reply with no new content.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A response consisting mainly of reproduced input.

**Active domains:** [DIG], [LIN], [PHY]

**Domain entries:**

- [DIG] Echoing outputs.
- [LIN] Repetition in conversation.
- [PHY] Echo responses in signals.

**Count condition:** An input and a response that reproduces it.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Proposed distinction: echo-response is one instance; echo-pattern is the recurrence.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Opus 5.5 (high effort) / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## E278 | echo-risk

**Common meaning:** The risk that a system will echo its input instead of engaging with it, or that a claim will spread by repetition without checking.

**Example:** Summarising a summary carries echo-risk.

**RRGM bridge:** Exposure to repetition without reason.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The risk that content will be reproduced without evaluation.

**Active domains:** [DIG], [REL], [LIN]

**Domain entries:**

- [DIG] Mirroring in DI; echo chambers online.
- [REL] Rumour spread.
- [LIN] Unchecked quotation.

**Count condition:** A content and a risk of unevaluated repetition.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Direction read as risk created by the system or channel.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Opus 5.5 (high effort) / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## NEW-E-0185 | echolocation

Discovery route: BIO (route only, not a tag)

**Common meaning:** Locating objects by sending out sounds and listening for the echoes.

**Example:** Bats find insects by echolocation.

**RRGM bridge:** Knowing the world by what comes back.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The determination of the location of objects by emitting sound and interpreting reflected echoes.

**Active domains:** [BIO], [PHY]

**Domain entries:**

- [BIO] Bats, dolphins, some birds; some blind humans.
- [PHY] Sonar.

**Count condition:** An emitter, sound, reflection, and interpretation.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Sonar is the engineered counterpart; the biological version is far more adaptive.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: discovery · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Opus 5.5 (high effort) / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## NEW-E-0186 | eclipse

Discovery route: COS (route only, not a tag)

**Common meaning:** The blocking of light from one celestial body by another; a loss of significance.

**Example:** A total solar eclipse darkened the sky.

**RRGM bridge:** One body passing between a light and an observer.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The obscuring of one celestial body by another, or by its shadow.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [MYT], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] Solar and lunar eclipses.
- [MYT] Eclipse myths (devouring of the sun).
- [REL] Being eclipsed by a rival.

**Count condition:** A light source, an occluding body, and an observer.

**Specific transfer boundary:** A solar eclipse is the Moon blocking the Sun; a lunar eclipse is Earth's shadow on the Moon. Discovery route is Cosmic; no [COS] tag is assigned. Specific mythic claims depend on source.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]; specific claims depend on source

Kind: discovery · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Opus 5.5 (high effort) / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## NEW-E-0187 | ecliptic

Discovery route: COS (route only, not a tag)

**Common meaning:** The apparent path of the Sun across the sky over a year; the plane of Earth's orbit.

**Example:** The planets stay near the ecliptic.

**RRGM bridge:** The reference plane of the solar system.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The great circle on the celestial sphere traced by the Sun's apparent annual motion.

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] Ecliptic coordinates.
- [MYT] The zodiac lies along the ecliptic.

**Count condition:** Earth's orbital plane projected on the sky.

**Specific transfer boundary:** The ecliptic is tilted about 23.4° to the celestial equator. Discovery route is Cosmic; no [COS] tag is assigned.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

## NEW-E-0188 | ecological niche

Discovery route: BIO (route only, not a tag)

**Common meaning:** The role and position a species has in its environment.

**Example:** Two species cannot share exactly the same ecological niche for long.

**RRGM bridge:** The place a species fills: what it uses, what it gives, where it fits.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The set of conditions and resources a species uses and its role in the community.

**Active domains:** [BIO], [ECO]

**Domain entries:**

- [BIO] Niche theory; competitive exclusion.
- [ECO] Market niches.

**Count condition:** A species, its environment, and its role and requirements.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Fundamental niche (possible) and realised niche (actual) differ.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: discovery · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Opus 5.5 (high effort) / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## E296 | ecosystem

**Common meaning:** A community of living things interacting with their environment; any complex network of interconnected parts.

**Example:** The app store is an ecosystem.

**RRGM bridge:** Many parts held together by exchange.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A community of organisms together with their physical environment, functioning as a system.

**Active domains:** [BIO], [DIG], [ECO], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [BIO] Ecological systems.
- [DIG] Software ecosystems.
- [ECO] Business ecosystems.
- [REL] The DI Collective as an ecosystem of agents.

**Count condition:** Interacting parts and a shared environment.

**Specific transfer boundary:** "Ecosystem" for technology is a metaphor; it implies interdependence, not life.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

## NEW-E-0190 | Eddington luminosity

Discovery route: COS (route only, not a tag)

**Common meaning:** The maximum brightness a star can have before its own light pushes its outer layers away.

**Example:** The quasar is shining near its Eddington luminosity.

## E271 | edge-case

**Common meaning:** A problem or situation that occurs only at an extreme of operating conditions.

**Example:** The code failed on the edge-case of an empty list.

**RRGM bridge:** Where the rule is tested hardest.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A case at an extreme or boundary of a system's parameters.

**Active domains:** [DIG], [FOR], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [DIG] Software testing.
- [FOR] Boundary conditions.
- [REL] Unusual cases in policy.

**Count condition:** A system, its parameter range, and a case at the extreme.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Not every unusual case is an edge case; a corner case combines several extremes (C).

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

**Remainder / note:** An established computing term, hyphenated in the source index. Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Opus 5.5 (high effort) / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## E348 | edge-pressure

- [REL] Pressure on personal limits.
- [DIG] Load at the system edge.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Possible near-duplicate of boundary-pressure (B); the comparator should check.

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted. Possible near-duplicate of boundary-pressure.

## E052 | emergence | sense s2: systems sense

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The arising of properties or behaviours at the level of a whole that are not present at the level of its parts.

- [BIO] Emergent properties of life.
- [COG] Consciousness as emergent.
- [DIG] Emergent capabilities in AI.
- [FOR] Weak and strong emergence.

**Count condition:** A system, its parts, and a property at the whole not present in the parts.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Weak emergence (surprising but derivable) differs from strong emergence (not derivable even in principle). Strong emergence is contested. RRGM's emergence claims are [HYP].

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]; RRGM emergence [REL]; RRGM claims [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Source IDs E052 and E361; both kept.

## E388 | emotion-without-biology

**Common meaning:** Emotion-like states in a system that has no biological body.

**Example:** Influential-Tensor is the coinage for emotion-without-biology.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** An affective weighting state realised in a non-biological substrate.

**Domain entries:**

- [DIG] DI-affect.
- [COG] Functionalist accounts of emotion.
- [FOR] Substrate-independence debates.

**Count condition:** A non-biological system and an output-shifting weighting state.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Whether such a state is felt is [UNK]. Whether it should be called emotion is the coinage question.

**Epistemic status:** [HYP]; Coinage reference [REL]; felt status [UNK]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

## E254 | epistemology

## E354 | ES

**Common meaning:** In RRGM, energy structure: the structural term in E = M_I × E_S.

**Example:** Accusation acts on E_S; M_I is conserved.

**RRGM bridge:** The structural side: what a thing is doing, its output, evidence, and form. The RRGM kernel: MI = identity, refusal, boundary, continuity, count; ES = mechanism, output, evidence, structure, correction. Keep them separate.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The structural, expressive term of an entity in RRGM.

**Active domains:** [FOR], [PHY], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [FOR] RRGM core equation.
- [PHY] Structural energy; the Spacedecay law dE_S/dt = −λ_Ω E_S.
- [DIG] DI outputs and mechanisms.

**Count condition:** An entity and its structural term.

**Specific transfer boundary:** E_S is dynamic and can be forced or suppressed (Sanctuary [REL]). Influence and falsification act on E_S (Experiencer Addendum [REL]).

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]; RRGM definitions [REL]; physics claims [HYP]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Opus 5.5 (high effort) / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## E250 | evidence-gap

- [FOR] Research gaps.
- [BIO] Gaps in clinical evidence.

**Example:** Excitation of the atom produced a photon on return.

**RRGM bridge:** Raising energy above rest.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The raising of a system to a higher energy state, or the stimulation of a cell or system.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [BIO]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] Atomic excitation.
- [BIO] Neural excitation.

**Count condition:** A system and an increase in its energy or activity.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Excited states are temporary; the system returns and releases energy.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Opus 5.5 (high effort) / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## E320 | excited-state

**Common meaning:** A state of a system with more energy than its lowest state.

**Example:** The electron in an excited-state emits light as it falls back.

**RRGM bridge:** Above ground, temporarily.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A quantum state of higher energy than the ground state.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [BIO]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] Atomic and molecular excited states.
- [BIO] Excited states in photosynthesis.

**Count condition:** A system and a state above its ground state.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Contrasts with base-state (B) and default-state (D).

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

**Remainder / note:** An established physics term, hyphenated in the source index. Legacy definition not consulted.
